we can argue as in Remark
2.13
and consider its composition with
\mathsf{T}_{q}
, which is a radially
1
-homogeneous metric, obtaining

\mathscr{U}_{\varrho^{p}\circ\mathsf{T}_{q}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=\inf\left\{\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{\alpha}:\bm{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{H}^{q}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\right\}.

As a relevant example, the case
\varrho=\mathsf{d}_{\mathfrak{C}}
(see (
2.9
)) and
p=q=2
fits into this setting and the resulting functional
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}
is the Helliger-Kantorovich metric on non-negative measures introduced in
[
LMS18
]
.

The next two theorems are generalizations of
[
LMS18
, Corollary 7.14, Theorem 7.15]
and, although the proofs are similar, there are some modifications to be taken into account so that we report them. Similar results are also treated in
[
De 20
,
DM22
]
.
First of all we show that
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}
is indeed a metric.

Theorem A.4
.

Let
\varrho
be as in (
A.1
). Then
(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X}),\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p})
is an extended metric space and
(\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X}),\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p})
is a metric space.

Proof.

Define
\mathsf{T}:\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]\to\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]
as

\mathsf{T}(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{2})=(\mathfrak{y}_{2},\mathfrak{y}_{1}),\quad(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{2})\in\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}].

Then, for every
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X})
,
\mathsf{T}_{\sharp}:\mathfrak{H}^{1}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\to\mathfrak{H}^{1}(\mu_{2},\mu_{1})
is a bijection satisfying

\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{\alpha}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\mathsf{T}_{\sharp}\bm{\alpha}

by the symmetry of
\varrho
. This gives that
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{2},\mu_{1})
for every
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X})
.

If
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X})
and we define

\bm{\alpha}=((\text{id}_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]},\text{id}_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]})\circ\mathfrak{p})_{\sharp}(\mu\otimes\delta_{1})\in\mathfrak{H}^{1}(\mu,\mu),

we obtain that

\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}^{p}(\mu,\mu)\leq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\bm{\alpha}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{y})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathfrak{p}_{\sharp}(\mu\otimes\delta_{1}))(\mathfrak{y})=0.

If, on the other hand,
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X})
are s.t.
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=0
and
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{H}^{1}_{o}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
is optimal, we get that
\bm{\alpha}
is concentrated on the diagonal
\{(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{y}):\mathfrak{y}\in\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]\}
, so that
\mu_{1}=\mathfrak{h}^{1}_{1}(\bm{\alpha})=\mathfrak{h}_{2}^{1}(\bm{\alpha})=\mu_{2}
. This proves that
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=0
if and only if
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}
.

Finally if
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X})
, we can find, thanks to Proposition
3.22
,
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}])
such that

\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha_{i})=\mu_{i},\quad\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{i-1},\mu_{i})=W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{i-1},\alpha_{i})\quad i=2,3.

Using again Proposition
3.22
, we have

\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{3})\leq W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3})\leq W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})+W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})=\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})+\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{2},\mu_{3}).

This proves that
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}
satisfies the triangle inequality and concludes the proof that
(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X}),\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p})
is an extended metric space.

If
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X})
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}])
is s.t.
\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha)=\mu
, then

\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\alpha(\mathfrak{y})=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}r\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\alpha([x,r])=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<+\infty,

so that
\alpha\in\mathcal{P}_{\varrho,p}(\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}])
. Then, again from Proposition
3.22
, if
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X})
we can find
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{\varrho,p}(\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}])
s.t.
\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha_{i})=\mu_{i}
for
i=1,2
so that

\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\leq W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})<+\infty.

∎

The following theorem extends to the unbalanced setting the well known result for the Wasserstein distance (see e.g.
[
AGS08
, Remark 7.1.11]
).

Theorem A.5
.

Let
\varrho
be a metric in
\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]
inducing the topology
of
\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]
such that
\varrho^{p}
is radially
1
-homogeneous.
If
(\mu_{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X})
and
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X})
, then

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{n},\mu)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\begin{cases}\mu_{n}\rightharpoonup\mu\\
\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n}(x)\to\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\end{cases}.

In particular if
\mathsf{X}
is separable, also
(\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X}),\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p})
is separable.

Proof.

Let us first observe that
(
2.8
)
yields

(A.2)

\text{there exists $a>0$ such that }\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\geq a\quad\text{ for every }x\in\mathsf{X}.

We first prove the
\Rightarrow
implication. Notice that, denoting by
\bm{0}_{\mathsf{X}}
the null measure in
\mathsf{X}
, we have

(A.3)

\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\nu,\bm{0}_{\mathsf{X}})=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\nu(x)\quad\text{ for every }\nu\in\mathcal{M}_{\varrho,p}(\mathsf{X})

so that, by triangle inequality, we get

\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n}(x)=\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}^{p}(\mu_{n},\bm{0}_{\mathsf{X}})\to\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}^{p}(\mu,\bm{0}_{\mathsf{X}})=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x).

We show that
\mu_{n}\rightharpoonup\mu
by contradiction: assume that there exist
\xi\in\mathrm{C}_{b}(\mathsf{X})
and a (unrelabeled) subsequence s.t.

(A.4)

\inf_{n}\left|\int_{\mathsf{X}}\xi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n}-\int_{\mathsf{X}}\xi\,\mathrm{d}\mu\right|>0.

Observe that

\mu_{n}(\mathsf{X})\leq\frac{1}{a}\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n}(x)\to\frac{1}{a}\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<+\infty,

so that
R:=(\sup_{n}\mu_{n}(\mathsf{X})+\mu(\mathsf{X}))<+\infty
.
By Theorem
3.4
and Lemma
2.12
, we can find
(\bm{\alpha}_{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}_{R}[\mathsf{X},\mathsf{X}])
such that
\bm{\alpha}_{n}\in\mathfrak{H}^{1}_{o}(\mu_{n},\mu)
is optimal for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let us define
\alpha^{1}_{n}:=\pi^{1}_{\sharp}\bm{\alpha}_{n}
,
\alpha^{2}_{n}:=\pi^{2}_{\sharp}\bm{\alpha}_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha^{2}_{n})=\mu
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain by Lemma
2.9
the existence of a subsequence
k\mapsto n(k)
and
\alpha_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}_{R}[\mathsf{X}])
with
\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha_{2})=\mu
such that
\alpha^{2}_{n(k)}\rightharpoonup\alpha_{2}
. Moreover

\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\alpha^{2}_{n}=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{0})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\quad\text{ for every }n\in\mathbb{N},

giving that (see e.g.
[
AGS08
, Proposition 7.1.5]
)
W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha^{2}_{n(k)},\alpha_{2})\to 0
. Then

W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n(k)}^{1},\alpha_{2})\leq W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n(k)}^{1},\alpha_{n(k)}^{2})+W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n(k)}^{2},\alpha_{2})=\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{n(k)},\mu)+W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n}^{2},\alpha_{2})\to 0,

where we used Proposition
3.22
. Thus
W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n(k)}^{1},\alpha_{2})\to 0
and, in particular,
\alpha_{n(k)}^{1}\rightharpoonup\alpha_{2}
so that

(A.5)

\int_{\mathsf{X}}\xi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n(k)}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}\xi(x)r\,\mathrm{d}\alpha_{n(k)}^{1}([x,r])\to\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}\xi(x)r\,\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}([x,r])=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\xi\,\mathrm{d}\mu,

where we used that the map

[x,r]\mapsto r\xi(x)

belongs to
\mathrm{C}_{b}(\mathfrak{C}_{R}[\mathsf{X}])
and
\alpha^{1}_{n(k)}
is concentrated on
\mathfrak{C}_{R}[\mathsf{X}]
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Since (
A.5
) is a contradiction with (
A.4
), this concludes the proof of the
\Rightarrow
implication.

Let us prove the
\Leftarrow
implication. If
\mu=\bm{0}_{\mathsf{X}}
, we have already by (
A.3
) that
\mathcal{D}_{\varrho,p}(\mu_{n},\mu)\to 0
. Let us then assume that
m:=\mu(\mathsf{X})>0
. Up to passing to a (unrelabeled) subsequence, we can assume that
m_{n}:=\mu_{n}(\mathsf{X})\geq m/2>0
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let us define
\alpha_{n},\alpha\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}])
as

\alpha:=\mathfrak{p}_{\sharp}\left(m^{-1}\mu\otimes\delta_{m}\right),\quad\alpha_{n}:=\mathfrak{p}_{\sharp}\left(m_{n}^{-1}\mu_{n}\otimes\delta_{m_{n}}\right)\quad n\in\mathbb{N}.

It is easy to check that
\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha_{n})=\mu_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathfrak{h}^{1}(\alpha)=\mu
and
\alpha_{n}\rightharpoonup\alpha
. To conclude is then sufficient to show that
W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n},\alpha)\to 0
and then apply Lemma
3.22
. The
1
-homogeneity of
\varrho^{p}
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\alpha_{n}(\mathfrak{y})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{X}}m_{n}^{-1}\varrho^{p}([x,m_{n}],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{n}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to\infty}}{{\to}}\int_{\mathsf{X}}\varrho^{p}([x,1],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{X}}m^{-1}\varrho^{p}([x,m],\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}]}\varrho^{p}(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{o})\,\mathrm{d}\alpha(\mathfrak{y}),

and we get that
W_{\varrho,p}(\alpha_{n},\alpha)\to 0
applying
[
AGS08
, Proposition 7.1.5]
.
∎

Appendix B
The case of a cost function finite on an open cone

In this appendix we repeat the constructions of Section
4
under different assumptions on
\mathsf{H}
. As in Section
4
, we assume that
\mathsf{X}_{1}
and
\mathsf{X}_{2}
are compact and metrizable spaces. Given non negative numbers
q_{i}
,
i=1,2
, we define the open cone

U_{q_{1}q_{2}}:=\left\{([x_{1},r_{1}],[x_{2},r_{2}])\in\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}_{1},\mathsf{X}_{2}]\mid r_{2}>r_{1}q_{1},\,r_{1}>r_{2}q_{2}\right\}.

Using this notation, we assume that

(B.1)

\begin{split}\mathsf{H}:&\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}_{1},\mathsf{X}_{2}]\to[0,+\infty]\text{ is continuous, radially $1$-homogeneous and convex and }\\
&\text{that there exists non-negative numbers $q_{1},q_{2}$ such that }{\mathrm{D}}(\mathsf{H})=U_{q_{1}q_{2}}\text{ and }\\
&\lim_{r_{1}\downarrow q_{2}}\inf_{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}}\mathsf{H}([x_{1},r_{1}],[x_{2},1])=\lim_{r_{2}\downarrow q_{1}}\inf_{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}}\mathsf{H}([x_{1},1],[x_{2},r_{2}])=+\infty.\end{split}

Notice that, if
q_{1}=q_{2}=0
, we are simply assuming that
\mathsf{H}
is finite on the whole open cone.

Proposition B.1
.

Assume that
\mathsf{H}
is as in (
B.1
) and that
\mu_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X}_{i})
are such that

\mathsf{supp}\left(\mu_{i}\right)=\mathsf{X}_{i},\,i=1,2,\quad q_{1}<\frac{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})}{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})},\quad q_{2}<\frac{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})},

where
q_{i}
are as in (
B.1
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for every
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\Phi_{\mathsf{H}}
with

\int_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}\varphi_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{1}+\int_{\mathsf{X}_{2}}\varphi_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{2}\geq 0,

it holds

\varphi_{1}(x_{1})\leq C,\quad\varphi_{2}(x_{2})\leq C\quad\text{ for every }(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}

and there exists
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}
such that
\varphi_{1}(\bar{x}_{1})\geq-C
,
\varphi_{2}(\bar{x}_{2})\geq-C
.

Proof.

We start from the last claim for
\varphi_{1}
. Assume by contradiction that there exists a sequence
(\varphi_{1}^{j},\varphi_{2}^{j})_{j}\subset\Phi_{\mathsf{H}}
with
\int_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}\varphi_{1}^{j}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{1}+\int_{\mathsf{X}_{2}}\varphi_{2}^{j}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{2}\geq 0
such that
\max_{x_{1}\in\mathsf{X}_{1}}\varphi_{1}^{j}(x_{1})\to-\infty
. Let
(x_{1}^{j})_{j}\subset\mathsf{X}_{1}
be the sequence of points where the maxima are attained. We thus have

\varphi_{1}^{j}(x_{1}^{j})\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})+\int_{\mathsf{X}_{2}}\varphi_{2}^{j}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{2}\geq 0\quad\text{ for every }j\in\mathbb{N}

so that we can find
(x_{2}^{j})_{j}\subset\mathsf{X}_{2}
such that

\varphi_{2}^{j}(x_{2}^{j})\geq-\frac{\varphi_{1}^{j}(x_{1}^{j})\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})}\quad\text{ for every }j\in\mathbb{N}.

Since
(\varphi_{1}^{j},\varphi_{2}^{j})\in\Phi_{\mathsf{H}}
, we have

\varphi_{1}^{j}(x_{1}^{j})\left(r_{1}-\frac{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})}r_{2}\right)\leq\varphi_{1}^{j}(x_{1}^{j})r_{1}+\varphi_{2}^{j}(x_{2}^{j})\leq\mathsf{H}([x_{1}^{j},r_{1}],[x_{2}^{j},r_{2}])\quad\text{ for every }r_{1},r_{2}\geq 0.

We can assume, up to passing to a subsequence, that
(x_{1}^{j},x_{2}^{j})\to(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}
.
Thanks to (
B.1
) and the assumptions on
\mu_{i}
, we can find
\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2}>0
such that

\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\bar{r}_{1}],[x_{2},\bar{r}_{2}])<+\infty,\quad\bar{r}_{1}-\frac{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})}\bar{r}_{2}<0.

We thus have that

+\infty\leq\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\bar{r}_{1}],[x_{2},\bar{r}_{2}],

a contradiction with (
B.1
). The proof for
\varphi_{2}
is the same; we have thus proven that there exists a constant
D>0
independent of
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
and a point
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}
such that

\varphi_{1}(\bar{x}_{1})\geq-D,\quad\varphi_{2}(\bar{x}_{2})\geq-D.

Thus, if we set

C:=D+\max_{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}}\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\bar{r}_{1}],[x_{2},\bar{r}_{2}]),

where
\bar{r}_{1}
and
\bar{r}_{2}
are as above, we get that

\varphi_{1}(x_{1})\leq\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{1}}\left(\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\bar{r}_{1}],[\bar{x}_{2},\bar{r}_{1}])-\bar{r}_{2}\varphi_{2}(\bar{x}_{2})\right)\leq C\quad\text{ for every }x_{1}\in\mathsf{X}_{1}

and the corresponding statement for
\varphi_{2}
.
∎

In the following result, which is the analogue of Proposition
4.6
, we use the notation of Definition
4.5
.

Proposition B.2
.

Assume that
\mathsf{H}
is as in (
B.1
) and that
\mu_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X}_{i})
are such that

\mathsf{supp}\left(\mu_{i}\right)=\mathsf{X}_{i},\,i=1,2,\quad q_{1}<\frac{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})}{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})},\quad q_{2}<\frac{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})},

where
q_{i}
are as in (
B.1
). Then there exist constants
a_{i},a_{s},b_{i},b_{s},M>0
such that, if
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\Phi_{\mathsf{H}}
are such that

\int_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}\varphi_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{1}+\int_{\mathsf{X}_{2}}\varphi_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{2}\geq 0,

then
\|\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}\|_{\infty},\|\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{H}}\|_{\infty}\leq M
and

(B.2)

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}(x_{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{x_{1}\in\mathsf{X}_{1}}\inf_{a_{i}\leq\alpha\leq a_{s}}\biggl\{\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\alpha],[x_{2},1])-\alpha\varphi_{1}(x_{1})\biggr\},\quad x_{2}\in\mathsf{X}_{2},

(B.3)

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{H}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\inf_{x_{2}\in\mathsf{X}_{2}}\inf_{b_{i}\leq\alpha\leq b_{s}}\biggl\{\mathsf{H}([x_{1},1],[x_{2},\alpha])-\alpha\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}(x_{2})\biggr\},\quad x_{1}\in\mathsf{X}_{1}.

Moreover the sets

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\{(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{2})\in\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}_{1},\mathsf{X}_{2}]\mid a_{i}\leq\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{1})\leq a_{s},\,\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{2})=1\},

\displaystyle\quad\mathfrak{C}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle:=\{(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{2})\in\mathfrak{C}[\mathsf{X}_{1},\mathsf{X}_{2}]\mid b_{i}\leq\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{2})\leq b_{s},\,\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{1})=1\}

are compact subset of
U_{q_{1}q_{2}}
and
(\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{H}},\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}})\in\Phi_{\mathsf{H}}
,
\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{H}}\geq\varphi_{1}
,
\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}\geq\varphi_{2}
. Finally, if
\mathsf{d}_{i}
are distances metrizing
\mathsf{X}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, then
\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{H}}
is
\mathsf{d}_{1}
-uniformly continuous with the same (uniform)
\mathsf{d}_{1}\otimes_{\mathfrak{C}}\mathsf{d}_{2}
-modulus of continuity of
\mathsf{H}
on
\mathfrak{C}_{0}^{2}
and
\varphi_{1}^{\mathsf{H}}
is
\mathsf{d}_{2}
-uniformly continuous with the same (uniform)
\mathsf{d}_{1}\otimes_{\mathfrak{C}}\mathsf{d}_{2}
-modulus of continuity of
\mathsf{H}
on
\mathfrak{C}_{0}^{1}
.

Proof.

Let
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
be as in the statement. Let us set

\delta:=\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-q_{1}q_{2}}{1+q_{1}+q_{2}}

so that, for every
0<\varepsilon\leq\delta
, we have
q_{2}+\varepsilon<(q_{1}+\varepsilon)^{-1}
. Let us fix a point
\bar{\alpha}\in(q_{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{q_{1}+\delta})
and let us define

m:=\max_{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}}\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\bar{\alpha}],[x_{2},1])<+\infty,

since
([x_{1},\bar{\alpha}],[x_{2},1])\in U_{q_{1}q_{2}}
for every
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}
.

By (
B.1
), we know that for every
L>0
, there exists
\varepsilon_{L}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}([x_{1},r_{1}],[x_{2},1]

\displaystyle\geq L\quad\text{ for every }(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2},\,0\leq r_{1}<q_{2}+\varepsilon_{L},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}([x_{1},1],[x_{2},r_{2}]

\displaystyle\geq L\quad\text{ for every }(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2},\,0\leq r_{2}<q_{1}+\varepsilon_{L}.

Let

L:=\max\left\{m+C(\bar{\alpha}+q_{2}+\delta),(q_{1}+\delta)(m+\bar{\alpha}C)+C\right\},

where
C
comes from Proposition
B.1
, and let us take any
a_{i},a_{s}>0
such that

q_{2}<a_{i}<q_{2}+\varepsilon_{L}\wedge\delta,\quad\frac{1}{q_{1}+\varepsilon_{L}\wedge\delta}<a_{s}<\frac{1}{q_{1}},

so that
0<a_{i}<a_{s}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{0}^{1}\subset U_{q_{1}q_{2}}
and
a_{i}\leq\bar{\alpha}\leq a_{s}
.

If
\alpha>a_{s}
, then, for every
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\alpha],[x_{2},1])-\alpha\varphi_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\alpha\left(\mathsf{H}([x_{1},1],[x_{2},1/\alpha])-\varphi_{1}(x_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\alpha(L-C)

\displaystyle\geq a_{s}(L-C)

\displaystyle\geq m+\bar{\alpha}C

\displaystyle\geq\mathsf{H}([\bar{x}_{1},\bar{\alpha}]),[x_{2},1]-\bar{\alpha}\varphi_{1}(\bar{x}_{1}),

where
\bar{x}_{1}
comes from Proposition
B.1
. If
\alpha<a_{i}
, then, for every
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathsf{X}_{1}\times\mathsf{X}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\alpha],[x_{2},1])-\alpha\varphi_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\geq L-\alpha C

\displaystyle\geq L-Ca_{i}

\displaystyle\geq m+\bar{\alpha}C

\displaystyle\geq\mathsf{H}([\bar{x}_{1},\bar{\alpha}],[x_{2},1])-\bar{\alpha}\varphi_{1}(\bar{x}_{1}).

Thus, for every
x_{2}\in\mathsf{X}_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\inf_{x_{1}\in\mathsf{X}_{1}}\inf_{0\leq\alpha<a_{i}\vee\alpha>a_{s}}\left\{\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\alpha],[x_{2},1])-\alpha\varphi_{1}(x_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\mathsf{H}([\bar{x}_{1},\bar{\alpha}],[x_{2},1])-\bar{\alpha}\varphi_{1}(\bar{x}_{1})

\displaystyle>\inf_{x_{1}\in\mathsf{X}_{1}}\inf_{a_{i}\leq\alpha\leq a_{s}}\left\{\mathsf{H}([x_{1},\alpha],[x_{2},1])-\alpha\varphi_{1}(x_{1})\right\}

and this proves (
B.2
). The proof of (
B.3
) is analogous.

The remaining part of the proof is identical to the one of Proposition
4.6
.
∎

By Proposition
B.2
we obtain the analogue of Theorem
4.7
also in this setting with exactly the same proof.

Theorem B.3
.

Assume that
\mathsf{H}
is as in (
B.1
) and that
\mu_{i}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathsf{X}_{i})
are such that

\mathsf{supp}\left(\mu_{i}\right)=\mathsf{X}_{i},\,i=1,2,\quad q_{1}<\frac{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})}{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})},\quad q_{2}<\frac{\mu_{1}(\mathsf{X}_{1})}{\mu_{2}(\mathsf{X}_{2})},

where
q_{i}
are as in (
B.1
). Then there exists
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\Phi_{\mathsf{H}}
such that

\int_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}\varphi_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{1}+\int_{\mathsf{X}_{2}}\varphi_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{2}=\mathscr{U}_{\mathsf{H}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}).
